# v1.8 Plan B results: CLMBR-T in place of, or added to, the ECG (33 trials; exploratory)

**Plan and code.** The plan is `docs/v17/V18_PLANS.md` Plan B, fixed before results. The leakage check (`CLMBR_LEAKAGE.md`) was committed before any result. Code is `scripts/v18/v18_clmbr.py`; outputs are in `audits/claude-v18-clmbr/`. Figure: `CLMBR_vs_ECG.png`.

**Reproduction.** Every base, ECG, shufECG, noise32 and unmatched row was compared with `claude-v17-confirm/results_all.csv` (P1, P2) and `results_p5.csv` (P5): 99 rows per PS × arm. The v1.7 cells reproduce **exactly: max deviation 0.0, 0 NaN mismatches**.

## Verdict in plain language

1. **Does CLMBR improve balance on non-coded physiology? Yes, strongly and robustly.** The endpoint is vitals, labs and echo only; the coded-record variables are excluded.
   - With a demographics PS (P1), the share of non-coded physiology variables with |SMD| < 0.1 rises from 53.3% to 61.6%: +8.4 pp, 26/33 trials, exact sign-flip p < 0.0001.
   - The gain is robust: comparator-cluster p = 0.0005, both split halves p ≤ 0.0003, LOO max p = 0.0001.
   - Shuffled CLMBR (+0.0 pp) and noise64 (−1.8 pp) do nothing.
   - The gain persists with thicker PSs: P5 +4.8 pp (p = 0.003), P2 +6.2 pp (p < 0.001).
   - On the covars2b lab/vital panel, which excludes all code-derived domains, CLMBR is dramatically better: 56% → 81%, 33/33 trials, vs ECG 56% → 61%. With values only (no measurement-presence indicators) it is 59% → 72%.
   - Caveat: CLMBR sees *which* labs were ordered (measurement codes) but not their values. Part of its lab balance is therefore "which tests were done" information.
2. **CLMBR vs ECG.**
   - On non-coded physiology, CLMBR ≈ ECG with a demographics PS: +2.2 pp in CLMBR's favour, two-sided p = 0.22. The same holds with P5: +1.0 pp, p = 0.49.
   - CLMBR is better with P2: +3.4 pp, p = 0.011.
   - On the full 58-panel and the lab panel, CLMBR is clearly better.
3. **Does it narrow the RCT gap? Yes on average, but not trial-specifically.**
   - P1 |Δlog HR| falls from 0.258 to 0.157 (22/33, p = 0.002, cluster p = 0.017).
   - z² falls from 8.2 to 2.4, and consistency with the RCT rises from 55% to 79%.
   - But the **benchmark shuffle fails** (p = 0.60 for |Δ|; 0.93 for z²). The same thing happens for P5 (0.32) and P2 (0.22).
   - So the RCT-gap reduction is generic shrinkage of the emulated HRs toward the null, not trial-specific agreement. This is the same pattern as the ECG in v1.7 (here ECG shuffle p = 0.38).
4. **Is ECG additive to CLMBR?**
   - **Balance: modestly, with thin PSs.**
     - P1: CLMBR+ECG vs CLMBR is +2.6 pp non-coded, 21/33, p = 0.003, cluster p = 0.012, both halves significant. Against the dimension-matched placebo (CLMBR+shufECG) it is +1.9 pp, p = 0.056.
     - P5: +2.9 pp, p = 0.024; vs placebo p = 0.24.
     - P2: none (−0.1 pp).
   - **Emulation: no.** |Δ| is 0.154 vs 0.157 (p = 0.41, shuffle p = 0.81).
   - CLMBR+ECG vs ECG: CLMBR adds to ECG on every balance endpoint (P1 non-coded +4.8 pp, p = 0.002).
5. **Which categories?**
   - **AF** has the largest CLMBR balance gain: +21.5 pp non-coded, 7/7 trials, p = 0.008. It is twice the ECG gain (+10.3 pp), and CLMBR beats ECG 7/7 (p = 0.016).
   - For the **AF RCT gap**, CLMBR shrinks it more (0.437 → 0.210, p = 0.047) but is not trial-specific (shuffle p = 0.89). The ECG AF gain *is* trial-specific (0.437 → 0.317, 7/7, shuffle p = 0.028, reproducing v1.7).
   - **HF**: no significant CLMBR gain on balance or gap.
   - **DM**: nothing.
   - **HTN and Other**: the gap shrinks (P1 HTN p = 0.062; P5 Other 5/5, p = 0.031), but not trial-specifically.
   - **ACS/post-MI**: n = 2, uninformative.
6. **Costs and leakage sensitivity.**
   - **Matching cost:** CLMBR matching keeps ~93% of the base pairs; ECG keeps 99.7%.
   - **S_noown** (27 trials) and **S_noexpo** (23 trials) remove trials where exposure-class tokens reach the model. The results are unchanged:
     - P1 non-coded CLMBR vs base: +7.9 pp (p < 0.001) and +6.2 pp (p = 0.003);
     - gap shuffle p = 0.66 / 0.56;
     - ECG added to CLMBR: p = 0.001 / 0.003.
   - **Set split:** in the v1.7 new-15 trials alone, the P1 CLMBR balance gain is smaller (+4.4 pp, p = 0.063). The ECG gain there is +5.7 pp (p = 0.039). The CLMBR effect is driven more by the v1.6 18 (+11.6 pp).

**Bottom line.** The code-based foundation-model embedding matches or exceeds the ECG for balancing unmeasured physiology. It clearly exceeds it for labs. Its RCT-agreement gains are, like the ECG's, generic shrinkage rather than trial-specific. ECG adds a small, placebo-borderline increment in physiology balance on top of CLMBR when the PS is thin, and nothing for emulation. The one trial-specific signal in the programme, AF with a demographics PS, belongs to the ECG, not to CLMBR.

## Definitions

- **PS designs:**
  - P1 = demographics (age, sex, index year);
  - P5 = P1 + HTN, T2D, CAD, AF, HF (`s11_p5.py`);
  - P2 = P5 + obesity (v1.7).
- **Arms:** base, +CLMBR64 (`T.clm_pc`), +ECG32, +CLMBR64+ECG32, +shufCLMBR (`T.clm_pc[T.shuffle_perm]`), +noise64. The noise64 arm is N(0,1), `np.random.default_rng(640000 + i)`, with i = trial index in ALL.
- **Placebos for the ECG contrasts:** shufECG, noise32, CLMBR+shufECG and ECG+shufCLMBR.
- **Estimator:** 1:1 greedy caliper-0.2 matching on the L2 logistic PS (C = 1), pair-clustered Cox.
- **Balance panels:**
  - (i) full 58-panel;
  - (ii) **non-coded subset** = the 54 variables in the groups Vitals & core labs, Other labs and Echo:* (the "Coded record" group is excluded). This is the PRIMARY endpoint.
  - (iii) covars2b non-proximal panel restricted to domain lab_vital, with the v1.7 per-PS exclusions; (iii-b) the same without measurement-presence indicators.
- **Tests:**
  - one-sided exact sign-flip across trials; two-sided for CLMBR vs ECG;
  - comparator-cluster sign-flip (`trial_selection.json` clusters);
  - LOO max p;
  - halves A/B;
  - benchmark shuffle: RCT HRs drawn without replacement from all 33 RCTs, 10,000 draws, p = P(null ≤ observed).
- **Multiplicity:** there is no correction; all cells are reported.

## Full tables

`a − b` < 0 is better for |Δ|, z² and mean |SMD|; > 0 is better for % |SMD| < 0.1 and consistency. p is in the direction "a better" (two-sided for CLMBR vs ECG). The complete CSVs are in `audits/claude-v18-clmbr/`: `summary.csv`, `per_category.csv`, `per_trial.csv`, `leakage.csv` and `verify_all.csv`.
